  SymGetOptions
  SymSetOptions
  The Compressor1 plug-in provides a means for dynamic range reduction of the signal. When the mean magnitude of the signal exceeds some settable threshold, a gain term is applied to the signal to reduce the overall amplitude. This gain will only cut in fully after a specified attack time, and conversely, will only be fully released when the mean magnitude is below the threshold (including hysteresis) for a specified release time. The gain amount is based on a settable ratio, and can be derived on a per channel basis, or can be based on the maximum mean magnitude channel across all channels. The punchiness characteristic of some compressors can be achieved by setting a relatively longer attack time ( > 1 second), while the sustain of certain compressors can be achieved by a longer release time. It should be noted that since the compressor threshold must be exceeded by the signal mean magnitude (via a lowpass filter with very low cutoff), in order to ensure that signal peaks that far exceed the mean are also compressed, a threshold that is much lower than those peaks must be selected. This might require a certain amount of experimentation. 
  The Pan3D plug-in allows for spatializing a mono signal over a 5.1 channel configuration. This plug-in is intended for use with the RWAudio3D library. RWAudio3D is responsible for producing a set of parameters or DSP coefficients which is to be used by Pan3D in processing the audio input signal.
  The Pan3D plug-in consists of a multi-tap delay line, a number of filters, and a number of Vector Base Amplitude Panning (VBAP) panners. The length of the delay line is determined by the constructor parameter maxDelaySeconds, and the number of read taps is determined by the number of reflections (a parameter in the DSPSettings struct) plus 1 for the direct path. The positions of the read taps are determined by the delay values of the direct and reflected paths. The input audio signal is first read either directly from a tap, or via interpolation in the case of a changing delay and corresponding Doppler shift. It then passes through a low pass filter, followed by a low shelving filter in the case of the direct path only, and finally into an improved VBAP panner. The low pass filter simulates air and surface absorption effects while the low shelving filter models the proximity effect. The VBAP panner maps the direct path azimuth to gain values for each of the 5 main channels in the 5.1 setup to allow for a smooth panning transition when the sound emitter is very close to the listener. The reflected paths use 2 channel panning on the assumption that reflections are normally far away, thereby reducing the amount of memory needed.
  The ReverbIR1 plug-in provides an efficient method for applying the reverberation characteristic of a given space to the game audio signal. This is achieved by performing the fast convolution of the signal with the impulse response (captured or created) of a given reverberant space. The efficient implementation is achieved by performing the convolution in the frequency domain, after having first sectioned the impulse response into smaller pieces in order to minimize delay and memory usage. To apply a given impulse response, a given impulse response file (such as say a stereo .wav file that contains the IR) must first be converted to an irf file (via SIMEX/SX). This file must be at the same sample rate as the output mode used by the game. The response is then applied to the signal via the EVENT_APPLYREVERB event. While the PlugIn has been designed for efficient operation, it does still require considerable resources, especially memory. To help mitigate this, memory usage can be traded off against the high frequency content of the reverberated signal retained. This is specified when creating the impulse response file (irf) via the SIMEX/SX parameter percentHighFrequencyCut. Reductions in memory usage will be nearly proportional to the numberspecified (i.e., if 50.0 is specified, memory usage will go down by close to half). To also minimize cycle usage, the SIMEX/SX parameter samplesPerFFTBlock allows for specification of the FFT block (segment)size. Larger sizes mean more efficient processing, but also add delay. Sizes used by the FFT must be a power of 2, so any sizes specified that aren't will be forced to a power of 2. The ReverbIR1 plug-in supports several channel-impulse response file configurations. 1:1 source to output channel mappings (1, 2, 4, or 6 channels) are supported, where the impulse response file is either mono, or it is comprised of the same number of channels as the signal input. Also, a 1:n source to output channel configuration is supported (1, 2, 4, or 6 output channels), where the impulse response file is comprised of n channels.
  The matrixpanner plug-in is used to place a mono sound source on multiple speakers, where each speaker has a separate gain controller.  The objective is to create a sense of placement relative to the game player when playing sounds. The gain levels should be specified from 0.0 to 1.0.  Negative values are allowed to invert the signal.  At present, the only configurations supported are 1X2, 1X4 and 1X6. 
  The other contruction parameter that has a great bearing on the amount of processor used is the Efficiency setting. This setting also relates to how the algorithm that finds the peak correlation points operates. Specifically, the algorithm first looks at a coarse granularity for the peak correlation point (within the correlation window), and then refines this peak location and value by moving at single sample granularity towards the true peak by moving in the direction of positive slope. The Efficiency setting, which ranges from a minimum of 1 (EFFICIENCY_1) to a maximum of 10 (EFFICIENCY_10), sets the granularity of the coarse search. The default setting of EFFICIENCY_6 corresponds to a coarse granularity of 16 samples, which seems to provide a good balance between efficiency and audio quality for most signals.
  The start time can be used to determine when the sine wave will begin generating output. The resolution of the start time is sample accurate.
  This attribute allows for the specification of the time in seconds over which the compressor reaches full compression, given that the mean magnitude of the input has exceeded the threshold. The punchiness characteristic of some compressors can be achieved by setting a relatively longer attack time ( > 1 second).
  This attribute specifies the size of the reverberant space via the setting of the average linear dimension of that space (in meters). The simplest example is that of a square room, in which case the space size would be specified as the distance between opposite walls. More complex geometries should be estimated by determining the average distance between pairs of opposing reflecting surfaces. 
  This event results in the DSPSettings object location being passed to Pan3D. The object is also copied to local memory owned by the Pan3D. The reason for copying the DSPSettings object to Pan3D local memory is to minimize the time duration during which the applicationcreating and filling in the DSPSettings object (such as RWAudio3D) and Pan3D are accessingthe same memory space. Given this approach, the only time during which a conflict could occur is during this event, when the data of DSPSettings is being copied over. As such, it is essential that the RWAC lock (rw::audio::core::System::Lock()) be acquired by the application whenever it is modifying the DSPSettings object. In the case of RWAudio3D, for example, this would be whenever the application calls the Calculate() method of that package to update the DSPSettings object values. 
  This mode can only be set via EVENT_SETMODE on the PS3 and will only be respected when the PS3 OSD audio configuration is set to a stereo output mode.
  This output parameter is pointed to a C string containing an end-user friendly name for the given output mode specified. This string may be used in option screens for example to enforce a consistent output mode naming across titles.
  This parameter is a pointer to a DSPSettings object that contains all the direct and reflected path details necessary for Pan3D to render the audio input to a 3D space. Typically the DSPSetting object will have been created by a higher level 3D audio application such as RWAudio3D. The object pointed to must be minimally aligned to 16 bytes to facilitate successful DMA transfers (PS3) and efficient processing and memory handling on all platforms. 
  This parameter should point to the seek table file (.sek). loaded into memory.  This parameter is optional unless a seek time has been specified.
  This value sets the correlation peak detection algorithm coarse correlation step size or granularity to 1 sample. This means that a coarse correlation is done for every sample offset. It is the least efficient setting, but guarantees that the optimal cut/loop point will always be found.

  Used to control the effect manager and global effect settings.
  When a mode other than 5.1 is set, the Dac plugin will perform an industry standard fold-down to the quad, stereo or mono output mode. Even if the Dac is not performing the fold-down, the PC or console platform might be performing a fold-down on its end. Hard clipping can be introduced during the fold-down if the signal coming into the fold-down is loud. Although the hard clipping should not be as audible a problem as it might sound, it can be avoided by reducing the volume of the 5.1 signal before it goes into the Dac plugin. If a signal is normalized to full scale (+/- 1.0) before it enters the Dac plugin by a plugin such as a compressor (strongly suggested), then when folding down to quad reducing the signal to 58% (-4.7 dB) of it's original level will guarantee no hard clipping will occur. When folding down to stereo, 37% (-8.6 dB) will guarantee no clipping. In reality, hard clipping noise is not as noticeable as one might think, and it may also be difficult drive the 5.1 signal to produce the extreme configuration required that will give a maximum peak after fold-down. For this reason it is never suggested that the volume be reduced to entirely reduce clipping (because a lot of volume will be lost), but instead find a happy medium through common sense and experimentation (e.g. somewhere between 0 and -6 dB).

  A PS3 game that wishes to support PL2 is faced with one of two choices on how to know when to enable it. The game code could have a front end menu option to allow the user to select between stereo and PL2. This is however very ugly to the end user as they now have to configure audio output in more than one place. The option is also confusing as it doesn't force stereo or PL2 mode if the OSD is set to a 5.1 or 7.1 output mode. For these reasons a menu option is not recommended. The saner alternative is to decide up front if your game will always output either plain stereo or PL2. This would probably be the audio director's choice based on what they prefer.

  On the Wii, the Dac sends its software mixed output through AX AuxB callback when the Dac is started.  The Dac upon creation will initialize the Wii AI and AX libraries by calling AIInit() and AXInit().  This means that the Dac will also be shutting down AI and AX by calling AIReset() and AXQuit() during Dac release to ensure proper matching calls.  Games that use AI and AX outside of RWAC should beware of these calls made in the Dac release (i.e. when releasing the voice with the Dac plugin or when releasing the RWAC System.  Note that stopping the Dac does not shutdown the AI and AX).  In the case of the Wii Home Menu, the game code should pause the master voice before entering the Wii Home Menu.
